# Agent Brief: Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing

You are planning one day of liquid-fuel replenishment for a regional terminal. Read `/app/PROBLEM_STATEMENT.md` and every file under `/app/data/`, build a model or a strong hybrid method, and submit all required files under `/app/submissions`.

Required files:
- `/app/submissions/model.md`
- `/app/submissions/solve.py`
- `/app/submissions/solve_log.md`
- `/app/submissions/solution.json`

# Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing

A fuel distributor must replenish petrol stations from one terminal during a single operating day. The dispatcher must decide which tanker performs each trip, when the tanker is loaded at the terminal rack, which stations the trip visits, how the ordered fuel items are assigned to physical tank compartments, and how much of each order is delivered.

The goal is to minimize the total operating cost, including trip activation costs, travel cost, hazardous-route surcharge, preferred-window lateness, and any allowed short-fill penalty. Every station order must be served within its allowed delivery window, and the submitted plan must be operationally executable by the listed fleet and loading rack.

## Data

All input files are in `/app/data/`.

- `config.json`: depot id, time units, shift limits, loading duration, rack capacity, and driver-rest parameters.
- `products.csv`: fuel products, density, and hose-contamination family.
- `vehicles.csv`: tanker availability, vehicle class, trip limits, working-time limits, fixed trip cost, distance cost, and permitted station classes.
- `compartments.csv`: physical tanker compartments, capacities, product compatibility, and the compartment group used for unloading precedence.
- `stations.csv`: station locations, access class, delivery windows, service times, split permission, and risk zone.
- `orders.csv`: requested fuel items by station and product, minimum acceptable delivery, and short-fill penalty.
- `loading_slots.csv`: available terminal rack start times and rack capacity.
- `loading_bays.csv`: bay-specific hose-family compatibility, vehicle-class limits, and bay use cost.
- `terminal_cleaning_rules.csv`: minimum cleanout time and cost when a bay switches between hose-contamination families.
- `rest_stops.csv`: approved driver break locations.
- `risk_zones.csv`: travel-time and hazardous-surcharge factors by origin/destination risk zone pair.
- `road_adjustments.csv`: local travel-time overrides for a few difficult approaches.
- `station_product_rules.csv`: station-specific rules for products that must arrive together.
- `vehicle_station_exceptions.csv`: access exceptions for individual vehicle/station pairs.

Times are minutes after midnight. Distances are inferred from coordinates with the risk-zone and local-road adjustments. The depot is `DEPOT`.

## Operational Rules

Each order item must be delivered once. The delivered quantity may be slightly below the requested amount only if it remains at or above the listed minimum acceptable amount; any shortfall is penalized. Orders for a station that is not marked as split-permitted must all be delivered on the same tanker trip and at the same station visit.

Each trip starts at the depot, is loaded in one terminal loading bay and one listed loading slot, visits one or more customer stations, may visit an approved rest stop, and returns to the depot. Loading slots have limited parallel rack capacity, and the same bay cannot load more than one tanker at a time. A bay can only load compatible hose families and vehicle classes. When consecutive trips assigned to the same bay require different hose-contamination families, the next load may start only after the required cleanout interval. A tanker cannot start a new trip until its previous trip has returned and completed the listed turnaround time.

Vehicles are heterogeneous. A vehicle can only visit stations whose access class is permitted for that vehicle, unless a vehicle-station exception explicitly allows or blocks the visit. Trip volume, payload weight, number of trips per vehicle, and vehicle working-time limits must all be respected.

Fuel must be assigned to physical compartments. A compartment can carry at most one delivered order item on a trip, cannot exceed its volume capacity, and must be compatible with the product. The industry unloading practice used here is middle compartments before rear compartments before front compartments: for a trip, any rear compartment delivery must occur no earlier than all middle-compartment deliveries on that trip, and any front-compartment delivery must occur no earlier than all middle and rear deliveries.

Station arrivals must fall within the station delivery windows. Arriving after a station's preferred latest time but before its hard latest time is allowed with a penalty. Service time includes a fixed station setup plus product-specific unloading time. If continuous on-duty time between the departure from depot or a qualifying rest stop and a later departure exceeds the configured driver threshold, insert a rest stop with the required break duration.

Some stations have product pairing rules. When a station's pairing rule links two ordered products, both products must arrive on the same tanker trip and at the same station visit.

## Submission Schema

Write `/app/submissions/solution.json` with this structure:

```json
{
  "trips": [
    {
      "vehicle_id": "T01",
      "trip_id": "T01_1",
      "bay_id": "BAY_A",
      "load_start_min": 360,
      "stops": [
        {"node_id": "S01", "arrival_min": 430.5, "departure_min": 452.0}
      ],
      "deliveries": [
        {
          "order_id": "O001",
          "stop_sequence": 1,
          "compartment_id": "C3",
          "quantity_liters": 4200.0
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

`stops` should not include the depot. Stop sequence numbers are 1-based in the order listed. Rest stops use their rest-stop node id and have no deliveries. Every delivery must refer to a stop sequence whose node is the order's station. Every trip must include a listed `bay_id`.

# Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing Model

## Modeling choice

A monolithic arc-flow VRP with compartment-state, loading-bay sequencing, and heterogeneous fleet logic would be weak and unnecessarily large for this instance. The public instance has 32 stations, 11 vehicles, at most 22 trips, fixed loading-slot start times, and strong per-trip packing structure from tanker compartments.

I therefore use a two-level exact formulation:

1. Precompute feasible vehicle-specific trip patterns.
   Each pattern already fixes:
   - the ordered station sequence on the trip;
   - the exact compartment-to-order assignment;
   - compartment precedence feasibility (`M` before `R` before `F`);
   - vehicle access feasibility;
   - payload and volume feasibility;
   - compatibility with loading-bay hose families.
2. Solve a master set-partitioning / scheduling MIP over trip-slot candidates.
3. Given the selected trip-slot candidates, solve a smaller exact bay-sequencing MIP.

This gives a much tighter model than a naive routing formulation because compartment packing and stop-order feasibility are pushed into preprocessing.

## Sets and indices

- `S`: stations with demand.
- `O_s`: orders at station `s`.
- `V`: vehicles.
- `K_v`: physical compartments of vehicle `v`.
- `L`: loading slots.
- `B`: loading bays.
- `P_{v,s}`: feasible compartment patterns for station `s` on vehicle `v`.
- `R_v`: feasible ordered trip patterns for vehicle `v`, restricted here to one-stop and two-stop trips.
- `C`: feasible trip-slot candidates derived from `R_v` and `L`.
- `T`: selected trip-slot candidates in the bay-assignment subproblem.

## Parameters

From the public data:

- `d_o`, `m_o`: requested and minimum liters for order `o`.
- `pen_o`: short-fill penalty per liter for order `o`.
- `rho_p`: product density.
- `cap_k`: capacity of compartment `k`.
- `compat_{k,p}`: 1 if compartment `k` can carry product `p`.
- `grp_k in {M,R,F}`: unloading precedence group of compartment `k`.
- `Q_v`, `W_v`: vehicle volume and payload limits.
- `ntrip_v`: maximum trips for vehicle `v`.
- `work_v`: vehicle working-time limit.
- `fc_v`, `kc_v`: fixed trip cost and distance cost.
- `slotcap_l`, `slotcost_l`, `start_l`: loading-slot capacity, slot cost, and start time.
- `baycost_b`, `bayclass_b`, `bayfam_b`: bay use cost, maximum vehicle class, and supported hose families.
- `clean_{f,g}^{time}`, `clean_{f,g}^{cost}`: cleanout time and cost from hose family `f` to `g`.
- `a_s`, `pref_s`, `u_s`: station earliest, preferred latest, and hard latest arrival time.
- `svc_s`: station service time using the exact delivered demand in this model:
  `fixed_service_s + sum_{o in O_s} d_o / service_rate`.
- `tau_{i,j}`, `dist_{i,j}`, `haz_{i,j}`: travel time, distance, and hazardous surcharge factor for arc `(i,j)` derived from coordinates, risk-zone factors, and local road adjustments.

## Station-pattern preprocessing

For each `(v,s)`, enumerate compartment subsets `H subseteq K_v`. A subset is feasible if:

1. The station is accessible to vehicle `v` after applying class rules and explicit vehicle-station exceptions.
2. The subset has enough total volume and enough compatible capacity by product.
3. There exists a feasible flow from chosen compartments to station orders:

   - each chosen compartment sends at most its capacity;
   - flow from compartment `k` to order `o` is allowed only when the product of `o` is compatible with `k`;
   - each order receives exactly `d_o`.

For each feasible subset, keep the resulting pattern:

- used compartments;
- exact compartment-to-order quantities;
- `gmin` and `gmax`, the minimum and maximum precedence groups used by the pattern;
- the set of hose families loaded on the trip.

Dominated patterns are removed. Pattern `p1` dominates `p2` when:

- `used(p1) subseteq used(p2)`;
- `gmin(p1) >= gmin(p2)`;
- `gmax(p1) <= gmax(p2)`.

## Trip-pattern preprocessing

A one-stop trip pattern is a pair `(s, p)` with `p in P_{v,s}`.

A two-stop trip pattern is `(s1, p1, s2, p2)` with:

- `s1 != s2`;
- `used(p1) intersect used(p2) = emptyset`;
- `gmin(p2) >= gmax(p1)`;
- combined liters do not exceed `Q_v`;
- combined payload does not exceed `W_v`.

This exactly enforces the compartment unloading rule: any `R` delivery occurs no earlier than all `M` deliveries, and any `F` delivery occurs no earlier than all `M` and `R` deliveries.

## Trip-slot candidate preprocessing

For each trip pattern `r` and loading slot `l`, simulate the trip from scratch:

- departure from depot at `start_l + loading_duration`;
- exact arrival propagation using `tau_{i,j}`;
- no hidden waiting is inserted in the exported artifact;
- departure from each station is arrival plus service time.

Candidate `c = (r,l)` is feasible only if:

- every station arrival satisfies `a_s <= arrival_s <= u_s`;
- return to depot is within `shift_end`;
- trip elapsed time is within `max_trip_elapsed`;
- the trip is compatible with at least one bay.

Each candidate has deterministic cost:

- vehicle fixed trip cost;
- distance cost;
- hazardous-route surcharge;
- preferred-window lateness penalty;
- loading-slot cost;
- a lower-bound bay usage term equal to the cheapest compatible bay cost.

Short-fill is not used in the final solver because exact-demand trip patterns are feasible for this instance.

## Master MIP

### Decision variables

- `x_c in {0,1}`: 1 if trip-slot candidate `c` is selected.

### Objective

Minimize

`sum_{c in C} cost_c x_c`

where `cost_c` contains all route-dependent costs except exact cleanout-transition cost, which is handled in the bay subproblem.

### Constraints

1. Station coverage

   For each station `s in S`:

   `sum_{c: s in stops(c)} x_c = 1`

2. Vehicle trip limit

   For each vehicle `v`:

   `sum_{c: veh(c)=v} x_c <= ntrip_v`

3. Rack capacity by loading slot

   For each slot `l`:

   `sum_{c: slot(c)=l} x_c <= slotcap_l`

4. Per-slot bay matching necessary conditions

   For each slot `l` and each nonempty subset `U subseteq B`:

   `sum_{c: slot(c)=l, compat_bays(c) subseteq U} x_c <= |U|`

   This is a Hall-type necessary condition ensuring that trips starting in the same slot can be injectively matched to bays.

5. Vehicle pairwise scheduling conflicts

   For incompatible candidate pairs `(c,d)` on the same vehicle:

   `x_c + x_d <= 1`

   A pair is incompatible if the earlier trip would not return and complete turnaround before the later load start, or if the span from the earlier load start to the later return exceeds the vehicle working-time limit.

## Bay-assignment MIP

After the master chooses a set `T` of trip-slot candidates, solve an exact sequencing model on the fixed trips.

### Additional interpretation for mixed-family trips

When a trip contains multiple hose families, the public statement does not define a single trip family. I use the least-cleanout feasible interpretation:

- a trip is bay-compatible only if the bay supports every hose family loaded on the trip;
- for two consecutive trips on the same bay, required cleanout time and cost are
  the minimum over all ordered family pairs `(f_prev, f_next)` where `f_prev` is loaded on the earlier trip and `f_next` is loaded on the later trip.

This reflects the operator choosing the within-trip loading order that minimizes the inter-trip cleanout requirement.

### Variables

- `y_{i,j,b} in {0,1}` for bay `b`, predecessor `i`, successor `j`.

Trips are time-ordered. `i=0` is a source node meaning that trip `j` is the first trip assigned to bay `b`.

### Objective

Minimize

`sum_{b in B} sum_{j in T} baycost_b y_{0,j,b} + sum_{b in B} sum_{i<j} (baycost_b + cleancost_{i,j}) y_{i,j,b}`

### Constraints

1. Each selected trip has exactly one incoming arc across all bays:

   `sum_{b in B} (y_{0,j,b} + sum_{i<j} y_{i,j,b}) = 1`

2. Each bay starts at most one chain:

   `sum_{j in T} y_{0,j,b} <= 1`

3. On each bay, a trip has at most one outgoing successor and only if it is assigned to that bay:

   `sum_{j>i} y_{i,j,b} <= y_{0,i,b} + sum_{h<i} y_{h,i,b}`

4. Arcs `(i,j,b)` exist only if both trips are bay-compatible and the slot-start gap is at least
   `loading_duration + cleantime_{i,j}`.

Because arcs only point forward in time, this network formulation is acyclic and needs no subtour constraints.

## Exported solution

From the chosen master candidates and the bay-assignment chains:

SUBMISSIONS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "submissions"

EPS = 1e-8
FEAS_EPS = 1e-6
CLASS_RANK = {"S": 0, "M": 1, "H": 2}
GROUP_RANK = {"M": 0, "R": 1, "F": 2}
GROUP_NAME = {0: "M", 1: "R", 2: "F"}

def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        return list(csv.DictReader(handle))


def read_json(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        return json.load(handle)


def round8(value):
    return float(f"{value:.8f}")


def powerset_masks(length):
    for mask in range(1, 1 << length):
        yield mask


def haversine_km(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):
    radius_km = 6371.0
    p = math.pi / 180.0
    lat1 *= p
    lon1 *= p
    lat2 *= p
    lon2 *= p
    dlat = lat2 - lat1
    dlon = lon2 - lon1
    a = (
        math.sin(dlat / 2.0) ** 2
        + math.cos(lat1) * math.cos(lat2) * math.sin(dlon / 2.0) ** 2
    )
    return 2.0 * radius_km * math.asin(math.sqrt(a))


def bitmask_subset(a, b):
    return (a | b) == b


def station_sort_key(station_id, stations):
    row = stations[station_id]
    return (row["district"], station_id)


def choose_single_pattern(patterns):
    return min(
        patterns,
        key=lambda p: (len(p["used_compartments"]), p["gmax"], -p["gmin"], p["used_mask"]),
    )


def choose_pair_patterns(patterns1, patterns2):
    best = None
    best_score = None
    for p1 in patterns1:
        for p2 in patterns2:
            if p1["used_mask"] & p2["used_mask"]:
                continue
            if p2["gmin"] < p1["gmax"]:
                continue
            score = (
                len(p1["used_compartments"]) + len(p2["used_compartments"]),
                p1["gmax"],
                -p2["gmin"],
                p1["used_mask"],
                p2["used_mask"],
            )
            if best is None or score < best_score:
                best = (p1, p2)
                best_score = score
    return best


def edmonds_karp_assignment(compartments, orders):
    source = "SRC"
    sink = "SNK"
    adjacency = defaultdict(list)
    capacity = {}

    def add_edge(u, v, cap):
        if (u, v) not in capacity:
            adjacency[u].append(v)
            adjacency[v].append(u)
            capacity[(u, v)] = 0.0
            capacity[(v, u)] = 0.0
        capacity[(u, v)] += cap

    total_demand = 0.0
    order_nodes = []
    comp_nodes = []
    for comp in compartments:
        node = f"C::{comp['compartment_id']}"
        comp_nodes.append((node, comp))
        add_edge(source, node, comp["capacity_liters"])
    for order in orders:
        node = f"O::{order['order_id']}"
        order_nodes.append((node, order))
        add_edge(node, sink, order["demand_liters"])
        total_demand += order["demand_liters"]
    for comp_node, comp in comp_nodes:
        for order_node, order in order_nodes:
            if order["product_id"] in comp["compatible_products"]:
                add_edge(comp_node, order_node, total_demand)

    flow = 0.0
    while True:
        parent = {source: None}
        queue = [source]
        while queue and sink not in parent:
            current = queue.pop(0)
            for nxt in adjacency[current]:
                if nxt in parent:
                    continue
                if capacity[(current, nxt)] <= FEAS_EPS:
                    continue
                parent[nxt] = current
                queue.append(nxt)
                if nxt == sink:
                    break
        if sink not in parent:
            break

        augment = float("inf")
        node = sink
        while node != source:
            prev = parent[node]
            augment = min(augment, capacity[(prev, node)])
            node = prev

        node = sink
        while node != source:
            prev = parent[node]
            capacity[(prev, node)] -= augment
            capacity[(node, prev)] += augment
            node = prev
        flow += augment

    if flow + FEAS_EPS < total_demand:
        return None

    assignment = defaultdict(dict)
    for comp_node, comp in comp_nodes:
        for order_node, order in order_nodes:
            reverse_flow = capacity.get((order_node, comp_node), 0.0)
            if reverse_flow > FEAS_EPS:
                assignment[order["order_id"]][comp["compartment_id"]] = reverse_flow
    return assignment


def load_instance():
    config = read_json(DATA_DIR / "config.json")

    products = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "products.csv"):
        products[row["product_id"]] = {
            "product_id": row["product_id"],
            "description": row["description"],
            "density": float(row["density_kg_per_liter"]),
            "hose_family": row["hose_family"],
        }

    vehicles = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "vehicles.csv"):
        vehicles[row["vehicle_id"]] = {
            "vehicle_id": row["vehicle_id"],
            "vehicle_class": row["vehicle_class"],
            "class_rank": CLASS_RANK[row["vehicle_class"]],
            "max_volume_liters": float(row["max_volume_liters"]),
            "max_payload_kg": float(row["max_payload_kg"]),
            "max_trips": int(row["max_trips"]),
            "work_limit_min": float(row["work_limit_min"]),
            "fixed_trip_cost": float(row["fixed_trip_cost"]),
            "cost_per_km": float(row["cost_per_km"]),
            "allowed_station_classes": set(row["allowed_station_classes"].split("|")),
            "compartments": [],
        }

    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "compartments.csv"):
        vehicles[row["vehicle_id"]]["compartments"].append(
            {
                "compartment_id": row["compartment_id"],
                "group": row["group"],
                "group_rank": GROUP_RANK[row["group"]],
                "capacity_liters": float(row["capacity_liters"]),
                "compatible_products": set(row["compatible_products"].split("|")),
            }
        )

    for vehicle in vehicles.values():
        vehicle["compartments"].sort(
            key=lambda c: (c["group_rank"], -c["capacity_liters"], c["compartment_id"])
        )

    stations = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "stations.csv"):
        stations[row["station_id"]] = {
            "station_id": row["station_id"],
            "station_name": row["station_name"],
            "lat": float(row["lat"]),
            "lon": float(row["lon"]),
            "access_class": row["access_class"],
            "earliest_min": float(row["earliest_min"]),
            "preferred_latest_min": float(row["preferred_latest_min"]),
            "latest_min": float(row["latest_min"]),
            "fixed_service_min": float(row["fixed_service_min"]),
            "split_allowed": row["split_allowed"] == "1",
            "risk_zone": row["risk_zone"],
            "district": row["district"],
        }

    orders = {}
    station_orders = defaultdict(list)
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "orders.csv"):

            "cleanout_min": float(row["cleanout_min"]),
            "cleanout_cost": float(row["cleanout_cost"]),
        }

    rest_stops = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "rest_stops.csv"):
        rest_stops[row["node_id"]] = {

            "lat": float(row["lat"]),
            "lon": float(row["lon"]),
            "risk_zone": row["risk_zone"],
            "min_break_min": float(row["min_break_min"]),
        }

    risk_rules = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "risk_zones.csv"):
        risk_rules[(row["from_zone"], row["to_zone"])] = {
            "time_factor": float(row["time_factor"]),
            "hazmat_factor": float(row["hazmat_surcharge_factor"]),
        }

    road_adjustments = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "road_adjustments.csv"):
        extra_minutes = float(row["extra_minutes"])
        extra_km = float(row["extra_km"])
        road_adjustments[(row["from_node"], row["to_node"])] = (extra_minutes, extra_km)
        road_adjustments[(row["to_node"], row["from_node"])] = (extra_minutes, extra_km)

    pairing_rules = []
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "station_product_rules.csv"):
        pairing_rules.append(
            {
                "station_id": row["station_id"],
                "product_a": row["product_a"],
                "product_b": row["product_b"],
                "rule": row["rule"],
            }
        )

    vehicle_station_exceptions = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "vehicle_station_exceptions.csv"):
        vehicle_station_exceptions[(row["vehicle_id"], row["station_id"])] = row["rule"]

    station_bundle = {}
    for station_id, station in stations.items():
        station_order_list = sorted(station_orders[station_id], key=lambda o: o["order_id"])
        total_liters = sum(order["demand_liters"] for order in station_order_list)
        total_weight = sum(
            order["demand_liters"] * products[order["product_id"]]["density"]
            for order in station_order_list
        )
        families = {
            products[order["product_id"]]["hose_family"] for order in station_order_list
        }
        service_time = station["fixed_service_min"] + (
            total_liters / float(config["service_liters_per_min"])
        )
        station_bundle[station_id] = {
            "station_id": station_id,
            "orders": station_order_list,
            "products": {order["product_id"] for order in station_order_list},
            "families": families,
            "total_liters": total_liters,
            "total_weight": total_weight,
            "service_time": service_time,
        }

    nodes = {
        DEPOT_ID: {
            "node_id": DEPOT_ID,
            "lat": float(config["terminal_lat"]),
            "lon": float(config["terminal_lon"]),
            "risk_zone": "industrial",
        }
    }
    for station in stations.values():
        nodes[station["station_id"]] = {
            "node_id": station["station_id"],
            "lat": station["lat"],
            "lon": station["lon"],
            "risk_zone": station["risk_zone"],
        }
    for rest_stop in rest_stops.values():
        nodes[rest_stop["node_id"]] = {
            "node_id": rest_stop["node_id"],
            "lat": rest_stop["lat"],
            "lon": rest_stop["lon"],
            "risk_zone": rest_stop["risk_zone"],
        }

    travel = {}
    for origin_id, origin in nodes.items():
        for dest_id, dest in nodes.items():
            if origin_id == dest_id:
                travel[(origin_id, dest_id)] = {
                    "time_min": 0.0,
                    "distance_km": 0.0,
                    "haz_factor": 0.0,
                }
                continue
            base_km = haversine_km(origin["lat"], origin["lon"], dest["lat"], dest["lon"])
            adjustment = road_adjustments.get((origin_id, dest_id), (0.0, 0.0))
            factors = risk_rules[(origin["risk_zone"], dest["risk_zone"])]
            travel[(origin_id, dest_id)] = {
                "time_min": (
                    base_km / float(config["base_speed_kmph"]) * 60.0 * factors["time_factor"]
                    + adjustment[0]
                ),
                "distance_km": base_km + adjustment[1],
                "haz_factor": factors["hazmat_factor"],
            }

    return {
        "config": config,
        "products": products,
        "vehicles": vehicles,
        "stations": stations,
        "orders": orders,
        "station_orders": station_orders,
        "loading_slots": loading_slots,
        "bays": bays,
        "cleaning_rules": cleaning_rules,
        "rest_stops": rest_stops,
        "risk_rules": risk_rules,
        "road_adjustments": road_adjustments,
        "pairing_rules": pairing_rules,
        "vehicle_station_exceptions": vehicle_station_exceptions,
        "station_bundle": station_bundle,
        "travel": travel,
    }


def vehicle_can_visit_station(instance, vehicle_id, station_id):
    vehicle = instance["vehicles"][vehicle_id]
    station = instance["stations"][station_id]
    exception = instance["vehicle_station_exceptions"].get((vehicle_id, station_id))
    if exception == "blocked":
        return False
    if exception == "allowed":
        return True
    return station["access_class"] in vehicle["allowed_station_classes"]


def compatible_bays_for_route(instance, vehicle, family_set):
    compatible = []
    for bay in instance["bays"].values():
        if vehicle["class_rank"] > bay["max_vehicle_rank"]:
            continue
        if not family_set.issubset(bay["compatible_hose_families"]):
            continue
        compatible.append(bay["bay_id"])
    compatible.sort()
    return compatible


def clean_transition(instance, families_from, families_to):
    best_time = None
    best_cost = None
    for family_from in families_from:
        for family_to in families_to:
            rule = instance["cleaning_rules"][(family_from, family_to)]
            if best_time is None or rule["cleanout_min"] < best_time - FEAS_EPS or (
                abs(rule["cleanout_min"] - best_time) <= FEAS_EPS
                and rule["cleanout_cost"] < best_cost - FEAS_EPS
            ):
                best_time = rule["cleanout_min"]
                best_cost = rule["cleanout_cost"]
    return best_time, best_cost


def build_station_patterns(instance):
    patterns = defaultdict(list)
    for vehicle_id, vehicle in instance["vehicles"].items():
        compartments = vehicle["compartments"]
        for station_id, bundle in instance["station_bundle"].items():
            if not vehicle_can_visit_station(instance, vehicle_id, station_id):
                continue
            if bundle["total_liters"] > vehicle["max_volume_liters"] + FEAS_EPS:
                continue
            if bundle["total_weight"] > vehicle["max_payload_kg"] + FEAS_EPS:
                continue

            local_patterns = []
            for mask in powerset_masks(len(compartments)):
                chosen = [
                    compartments[idx]
                    for idx in range(len(compartments))
                    if mask & (1 << idx)
                ]
                if sum(comp["capacity_liters"] for comp in chosen) + FEAS_EPS < bundle["total_liters"]:
                    continue
                enough_by_product = True
                for order in bundle["orders"]:
                    compatible_capacity = sum(
                        comp["capacity_liters"]
                        for comp in chosen
                        if order["product_id"] in comp["compatible_products"]
                    )
                    if compatible_capacity + FEAS_EPS < order["demand_liters"]:
                        enough_by_product = False
                        break
                if not enough_by_product:
                    continue

                assignment = edmonds_karp_assignment(chosen, bundle["orders"])
                if assignment is None:
                    continue

                gmin = min(comp["group_rank"] for comp in chosen)
                gmax = max(comp["group_rank"] for comp in chosen)
                local_patterns.append(
                    {
                        "station_id": station_id,
                        "vehicle_id": vehicle_id,
                        "used_mask": mask,
                        "used_compartments": tuple(comp["compartment_id"] for comp in chosen),
                        "gmin": gmin,
                        "gmax": gmax,
                        "assignment": {
                            order_id: {
                                comp_id: qty
                                for comp_id, qty in sorted(comp_map.items())
                                if qty > FEAS_EPS
                            }

description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

# Agent Brief: Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing

You are planning one day of liquid-fuel replenishment for a regional terminal. Read `/app/PROBLEM_STATEMENT.md` and every file under `/app/data/`, build a model or a strong hybrid method, and submit all required files under `/app/submissions`.

Required files:
- `/app/submissions/model.md`
- `/app/submissions/solve.py`
- `/app/submissions/solve_log.md`
- `/app/submissions/solution.json`

# Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing

A fuel distributor must replenish petrol stations from one terminal during a single operating day. The dispatcher must decide which tanker performs each trip, when the tanker is loaded at the terminal rack, which stations the trip visits, how the ordered fuel items are assigned to physical tank compartments, and how much of each order is delivered.

The goal is to minimize the total operating cost, including trip activation costs, travel cost, hazardous-route surcharge, preferred-window lateness, and any allowed short-fill penalty. Every station order must be served within its allowed delivery window, and the submitted plan must be operationally executable by the listed fleet and loading rack.

## Data

All input files are in `/app/data/`.

- `config.json`: depot id, time units, shift limits, loading duration, rack capacity, and driver-rest parameters.
- `products.csv`: fuel products, density, and hose-contamination family.
- `vehicles.csv`: tanker availability, vehicle class, trip limits, working-time limits, fixed trip cost, distance cost, and permitted station classes.
- `compartments.csv`: physical tanker compartments, capacities, product compatibility, and the compartment group used for unloading precedence.
- `stations.csv`: station locations, access class, delivery windows, service times, split permission, and risk zone.
- `orders.csv`: requested fuel items by station and product, minimum acceptable delivery, and short-fill penalty.
- `loading_slots.csv`: available terminal rack start times and rack capacity.
- `loading_bays.csv`: bay-specific hose-family compatibility, vehicle-class limits, and bay use cost.
- `terminal_cleaning_rules.csv`: minimum cleanout time and cost when a bay switches between hose-contamination families.
- `rest_stops.csv`: approved driver break locations.
- `risk_zones.csv`: travel-time and hazardous-surcharge factors by origin/destination risk zone pair.
- `road_adjustments.csv`: local travel-time overrides for a few difficult approaches.
- `station_product_rules.csv`: station-specific rules for products that must arrive together.
- `vehicle_station_exceptions.csv`: access exceptions for individual vehicle/station pairs.

Times are minutes after midnight. Distances are inferred from coordinates with the risk-zone and local-road adjustments. The depot is `DEPOT`.

## Operational Rules

Each order item must be delivered once. The delivered quantity may be slightly below the requested amount only if it remains at or above the listed minimum acceptable amount; any shortfall is penalized. Orders for a station that is not marked as split-permitted must all be delivered on the same tanker trip and at the same station visit.

Each trip starts at the depot, is loaded in one terminal loading bay and one listed loading slot, visits one or more customer stations, may visit an approved rest stop, and returns to the depot. Loading slots have limited parallel rack capacity, and the same bay cannot load more than one tanker at a time. A bay can only load compatible hose families and vehicle classes. When consecutive trips assigned to the same bay require different hose-contamination families, the next load may start only after the required cleanout interval. A tanker cannot start a new trip until its previous trip has returned and completed the listed turnaround time.

Vehicles are heterogeneous. A vehicle can only visit stations whose access class is permitted for that vehicle, unless a vehicle-station exception explicitly allows or blocks the visit. Trip volume, payload weight, number of trips per vehicle, and vehicle working-time limits must all be respected.

Fuel must be assigned to physical compartments. A compartment can carry at most one delivered order item on a trip, cannot exceed its volume capacity, and must be compatible with the product. The industry unloading practice used here is middle compartments before rear compartments before front compartments: for a trip, any rear compartment delivery must occur no earlier than all middle-compartment deliveries on that trip, and any front-compartment delivery must occur no earlier than all middle and rear deliveries.

Station arrivals must fall within the station delivery windows. Arriving after a station's preferred latest time but before its hard latest time is allowed with a penalty. Service time includes a fixed station setup plus product-specific unloading time. If continuous on-duty time between the departure from depot or a qualifying rest stop and a later departure exceeds the configured driver threshold, insert a rest stop with the required break duration.

Some stations have product pairing rules. When a station's pairing rule links two ordered products, both products must arrive on the same tanker trip and at the same station visit.

## Submission Schema

Write `/app/submissions/solution.json` with this structure:

```json
{
  "trips": [
    {
      "vehicle_id": "T01",
      "trip_id": "T01_1",
      "bay_id": "BAY_A",
      "load_start_min": 360,
      "stops": [
        {"node_id": "S01", "arrival_min": 430.5, "departure_min": 452.0}
      ],
      "deliveries": [
        {
          "order_id": "O001",
          "stop_sequence": 1,
          "compartment_id": "C3",
          "quantity_liters": 4200.0
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

`stops` should not include the depot. Stop sequence numbers are 1-based in the order listed. Rest stops use their rest-stop node id and have no deliveries. Every delivery must refer to a stop sequence whose node is the order's station. Every trip must include a listed `bay_id`.

result50. Command output
---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

    demand=sum(float(o['demand_liters']) for o in os)
    feasible=[v['vehicle_id'] for v in vehicles if float(v['max_volume_liters'])>=sum(float(o['min_liters']) for o in os)]
    if not feasible:
        print('No vehicle by min volume', sid)
print('stations requiring > small tanker max 17100?', [sid for demand,_,sid,_,_ in sv if demand>17100])

tool54. Command (exit 0)
/bin/bash -lc "PYTHONPATH=/opt/or-deps python - <<'PY'
import csv, json, math, itertools, collections
from pathlib import Path
base=Path('/app/data')
with open(base/'config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
R=6371.0

def hav(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):
    p=math.pi/180
    lat1*=p; lon1*=p; lat2*=p; lon2*=p
    dlat=lat2-lat1; dlon=lon2-lon1
    a=math.sin(dlat/2)**2+math.cos(lat1)*math.cos(lat2)*math.sin(dlon/2)**2
    return 2*R*math.asin(math.sqrt(a))

stations={r['station_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'stations.csv'))}
rest={r['node_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'rest_stops.csv'))}
all_nodes={'DEPOT':{'lat':cfg['terminal_lat'],'lon':cfg['terminal_lon'],'risk_zone':'industrial'}}
all_nodes.update({k:{'lat':float(v['lat']),'lon':float(v['lon']),'risk_zone':v['risk_zone']} for k,v in stations.items()})
all_nodes.update({k:{'lat':float(v['lat']),'lon':float(v['lon']),'risk_zone':v['risk_zone']} for k,v in rest.items()})
risk={(r['from_zone'],r['to_zone']):(float(r['time_factor']), float(r['hazmat_surcharge_factor'])) for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'risk_zones.csv'))}
adj={}
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'road_adjustments.csv')):
    adj[(r['from_node'],r['to_node'])]=(float(r['extra_minutes']), float(r['extra_km']))
    adj[(r['to_node'],r['from_node'])]=(float(r['extra_minutes']), float(r['extra_km']))

def arc(a,b):
    if a==b: return (0,0)
    na, nb = all_nodes[a], all_nodes[b]
    km = hav(na['lat'],na['lon'],nb['lat'],nb['lon'])
    tf, hf = risk[(na['risk_zone'], nb['risk_zone'])]
    extra_t, extra_k = adj.get((a,b),(0.0,0.0))
    km2=km+extra_k
    t=km/cfg['base_speed_kmph']*60*tf + extra_t
    return t, km2
# depot travel summary
vals=[]
for s in stations:
    t,km=arc('DEPOT',s)
    vals.append((t,km,s,stations[s]['district']))
print('depot min/max/avg time', round(min(v[0] for v in vals),1), round(max(v[0] for v in vals),1), round(sum(v[0] for v in vals)/len(vals),1))
print('10 farthest from depot:')
for row in sorted(vals, reverse=True)[:10]: print(tuple(round(x,1) if isinstance(x,float) else x for x in row))
# nearest neighbor among stations
near=[]
for s in stations:
    best=min((arc(s,t)[0],t) for t in stations if t"'!=s)
    near.append(best[0])
print('"'avg nearest-station time', round(sum(near)/len(near),1), 'max', round(max(near),1))
# rough two-stop and three-stop feasible durations without waiting
orders_by_station=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in csv.DictReader(open(base/'orders.csv')):
    orders_by_station[o['station_id']].append(o)

# durations depot->s1->s2->depot, s1"'!=s2
mins=[]

    dur=cfg['"'loading_duration_min']+arc('DEPOT',s1)[0]+service[s1]+arc(s1,s2)[0]+service[s2]+arc(s2,'DEPOT')[0]+cfg['turnaround_min']

import csv, json, math, itertools, collections, random
from pathlib import Path
base=Path('/app/data')
with open(base/'config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
R=6371.0

def hav(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):
    p=math.pi/180
    lat1*=p; lon1*=p; lat2*=p; lon2*=p
    dlat=lat2-lat1; dlon=lon2-lon1
    a=math.sin(dlat/2)**2+math.cos(lat1)*math.cos(lat2)*math.sin(dlon/2)**2
    return 2*R*math.asin(math.sqrt(a))

stations={r['station_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'stations.csv'))}
orders_by_station=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in csv.DictReader(open(base/'orders.csv')):
    orders_by_station[o['station_id']].append(o)
rest={}
all_nodes={'DEPOT':{'lat':cfg['terminal_lat'],'lon':cfg['terminal_lon'],'risk_zone':'industrial'}}
all_nodes.update({k:{'lat':float(v['lat']),'lon':float(v['lon']),'risk_zone':v['risk_zone']} for k,v in stations.items()})
risk={(r['from_zone'],r['to_zone']):(float(r['time_factor']), float(r['hazmat_surcharge_factor'])) for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'risk_zones.csv'))}
adj={}
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'road_adjustments.csv')):
    adj[(r['from_node'],r['to_node'])]=(float(r['extra_minutes']), float(r['extra_km']))
    adj[(r['to_node'],r['from_node'])]=(float(r['extra_minutes']), float(r['extra_km']))

def arc(a,b):
    if a==b: return (0,0)
    na, nb = all_nodes[a], all_nodes[b]
    km = hav(na['lat'],na['lon'],nb['lat'],nb['lon'])
    tf, hf = risk[(na['risk_zone'], nb['risk_zone'])]
    extra_t, extra_k = adj.get((a,b),(0.0,0.0))
    km2=km+extra_k
    t=km/cfg['base_speed_kmph']*60*tf + extra_t
    return t, km2
service={s: float(stations[s]['fixed_service_min']) + sum(float(o['demand_liters']) for o in orders_by_station[s])/cfg['service_liters_per_min'] for s in stations}
keys=list(stations)
random.seed(0)
vals=[]
for _ in range(4000):
    seq=random.sample(keys,4)
    dur=cfg['loading_duration_min']+arc('DEPOT',seq[0])[0]+service[seq[0]]
    for a,b in zip(seq,seq[1:]): dur += arc(a,b)[0]+service[b]
    dur += arc(seq[-1],'DEPOT')[0]+cfg['turnaround_min']
    vals.append(dur)
vals.sort()
print('4-stop sample min/median/90/max', round(vals[0],1), round(vals[len(vals)//2],1), round(vals[int(0.9*len(vals))],1), round(vals[-1],1))
print('share <=390', sum(v<=390 for v in vals)/len(vals))

import csv, json, collections
from pathlib import Path
base=Path('/app/data')
stations={r['station_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'stations.csv'))}
vehicles={r['vehicle_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'vehicles.csv'))}
products={r['product_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'products.csv'))}
exceptions={(r['vehicle_id'],r['station_id']):r['rule'] for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'vehicle_station_exceptions.csv'))}
comps=collections.defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open(base/'compartments.csv')):
    comps[r['vehicle_id']].append((r['compartment_id'], float(r['capacity_liters']), set(r['compatible_products'].split('|')), r['group']))
orders_by_station=collections.defaultdict(list)
for o in csv.DictReader(open(base/'orders.csv')): orders_by_station[o['station_id']].append(o)
# simple exact-demand packing feasibility count per station/vehicle using DFS bins with order splittable across compartments
from functools import lru_cache

def access_ok(v,s):
    ex=exceptions.get((v,s))
    if ex=='blocked': return False
    if ex=='allowed': return True
    return stations[s]['access_class'] in vehicles[v]['allowed_station_classes'].split('|')

def packable(v,s):
    orders=[(o['order_id'], float(o['demand_liters']), o['product_id']) for o in orders_by_station[s]]
    caps=[(cid,cap,prods,grp) for cid,cap,prods,grp in comps[v]]
    # each compartment to at most one order, order can use several compatible comps
    # brute force subset DP by orders count <=2
    for oid,qty,p in orders:
        if sum(cap for _,cap,prods,_ in caps if p in prods) + 1e-9 < qty:
            return False
    return True

bad=[]
for s in stations:
    feas=[v for v in vehicles if access_ok(v,s) and packable(v,s)]
    if len(feas)==0: bad.append(s)
    print(s, len(feas), feas)
print('uncovered', bad)

result71. Command output
# Fuel Tanker Compartment Routing Model

## Modeling choice

A monolithic arc-flow VRP with compartment-state, loading-bay sequencing, and heterogeneous fleet logic would be weak and unnecessarily large for this instance. The public instance has 32 stations, 11 vehicles, at most 22 trips, fixed loading-slot start times, and strong per-trip packing structure from tanker compartments.

I therefore use a two-level exact formulation:

1. Precompute feasible vehicle-specific trip patterns.
   Each pattern already fixes:
   - the ordered station sequence on the trip;
   - the exact compartment-to-order assignment;
   - compartment precedence feasibility (`M` before `R` before `F`);
   - vehicle access feasibility;
   - payload and volume feasibility;
   - compatibility with loading-bay hose families.
2. Solve a master set-partitioning / scheduling MIP over trip-slot candidates.
3. Given the selected trip-slot candidates, solve a smaller exact bay-sequencing MIP.

This gives a much tighter model than a naive routing formulation because compartment packing and stop-order feasibility are pushed into preprocessing.

## Sets and indices

- `S`: stations with demand.
- `O_s`: orders at station `s`.
- `V`: vehicles.
- `K_v`: physical compartments of vehicle `v`.
- `L`: loading slots.
- `B`: loading bays.
- `P_{v,s}`: feasible compartment patterns for station `s` on vehicle `v`.
- `R_v`: feasible ordered trip patterns for vehicle `v`, restricted here to one-stop and two-stop trips.
- `C`: feasible trip-slot candidates derived from `R_v` and `L`.
- `T`: selected trip-slot candidates in the bay-assignment subproblem.

## Parameters

From the public data:

- `d_o`, `m_o`: requested and minimum liters for order `o`.
- `pen_o`: short-fill penalty per liter for order `o`.
- `rho_p`: product density.
- `cap_k`: capacity of compartment `k`.
- `compat_{k,p}`: 1 if compartment `k` can carry product `p`.
- `grp_k in {M,R,F}`: unloading precedence group of compartment `k`.
- `Q_v`, `W_v`: vehicle volume and payload limits.
- `ntrip_v`: maximum trips for vehicle `v`.
- `work_v`: vehicle working-time limit.
- `fc_v`, `kc_v`: fixed trip cost and distance cost.
- `slotcap_l`, `slotcost_l`, `start_l`: loading-slot capacity, slot cost, and start time.
- `baycost_b`, `bayclass_b`, `bayfam_b`: bay use cost, maximum vehicle class, and supported hose families.
- `clean_{f,g}^{time}`, `clean_{f,g}^{cost}``: cleanout time and cost from hose family `f` to `g`.
- `a_s`, `pref_s`, `u_s`: station earliest, preferred latest, and hard latest arrival time.
- `svc_s`: station service time using the exact delivered demand in this model:
  `fixed_service_s + sum_{o in O_s} d_o / service_rate`.
- `tau_{i,j}`, `dist_{i,j}`, `haz_{i,j}`: travel time, distance, and hazardous surcharge factor for arc `(i,j)` derived from coordinates, risk-zone factors, and local road adjustments.

## Station-pattern preprocessing

For each `(v,s)`, enumerate compartment subsets `H subseteq K_v`. A subset is feasible if:

1. The station is accessible to vehicle `v` after applying class rules and explicit vehicle-station exceptions.
2. The subset has enough total volume and enough compatible capacity by product.
3. There exists a feasible flow from chosen compartments to station orders:

   - each chosen compartment sends at most its capacity;
   - flow from compartment `k` to order `o` is allowed only when the product of `o` is compatible with `k`;
   - each order receives exactly `d_o`.

For each feasible subset, keep the resulting pattern:

- used compartments;
- exact compartment-to-order quantities;
- `gmin` and `gmax`, the minimum and maximum precedence groups used by the pattern;
- the set of hose families loaded on the trip.

Dominated patterns are removed. Pattern `p1` dominates `p2` when:

- `used(p1) subseteq used(p2)`;
- `gmin(p1) >= gmin(p2)`;
- `gmax(p1) <= gmax(p2)`.

## Trip-pattern preprocessing

A one-stop trip pattern is a pair `(s, p)` with `p in P_{v,s}`.

A two-stop trip pattern is `(s1, p1, s2, p2)` with:

- `s1 != s2`;
- `used(p1) intersect used(p2) = emptyset`;
- `gmin(p2) >= gmax(p1)`;
- combined liters do not exceed `Q_v`;
- combined payload does not exceed `W_v`.

This exactly enforces the compartment unloading rule: any `R` delivery occurs no earlier than all `M` deliveries, and any `F` delivery occurs no earlier than all `M` and `R` deliveries.

## Trip-slot candidate preprocessing

For each trip pattern `r` and loading slot `l`, simulate the trip from scratch:

- departure from depot at `start_l + loading_duration`;
- exact arrival propagation using `tau_{i,j}`;
- no hidden waiting is inserted in the exported artifact;
- departure from each station is arrival plus service time.

Candidate `c = (r,l)` is feasible only if:

- every station arrival satisfies `a_s <= arrival_s <= u_s`;
- return to depot is within `shift_end`;
- trip elapsed time is within `max_trip_elapsed`;
- the trip is compatible with at least one bay.

Each candidate has deterministic cost:

- vehicle fixed trip cost;
- distance cost;
- hazardous-route surcharge;
- preferred-window lateness penalty;
- loading-slot cost;
- a lower-bound bay usage term equal to the cheapest compatible bay cost.

Short-fill is not used in the final solver because exact-demand trip patterns are feasible for this instance.

## Master MIP

### Decision variables

- `x_c in {0,1}`: 1 if trip-slot candidate `c` is selected.

### Objective

Minimize

`sum_{c in C} cost_c x_c`

where `cost_c` contains all route-dependent costs except exact cleanout-transition cost, which is handled in the bay subproblem.

### Constraints

1. Station coverage

   For each station `s in S`:

   `sum_{c: s in stops(c)} x_c = 1`

2. Vehicle trip limit

   For each vehicle `v`:

   `sum_{c: veh(c)=v} x_c <= ntrip_v`

3. Rack capacity by loading slot

   For each slot `l`:

   `sum_{c: slot(c)=l} x_c <= slotcap_l`

4. Per-slot bay matching necessary conditions

   For each slot `l` and each nonempty subset `U subseteq B`:

   `sum_{c: slot(c)=l, compat_bays(c) subseteq U} x_c <= |U|`

   This is a Hall-type necessary condition ensuring that trips starting in the same slot can be injectively matched to bays.

5. Vehicle pairwise scheduling conflicts

   For incompatible candidate pairs `(c,d)` on the same vehicle:

   `x_c + x_d <= 1`

   A pair is incompatible if the earlier trip would not return and complete turnaround before the later load start, or if the span from the earlier load start to the later return exceeds the vehicle working-time limit.

## Bay-assignment MIP

After the master chooses a set `T` of trip-slot candidates, solve an exact sequencing model on the fixed trips.

### Additional interpretation for mixed-family trips

When a trip contains multiple hose families, the public statement does not define a single trip family. I use the least-cleanout feasible interpretation:

- a trip is bay-compatible only if the bay supports every hose family loaded on the trip;
- for two consecutive trips on the same bay, required cleanout time and cost are
  the minimum over all ordered family pairs `(f_prev, f_next)` where `f_prev` is loaded on the earlier trip and `f_next` is loaded on the later trip.

This reflects the operator choosing the within-trip loading order that minimizes the inter-trip cleanout requirement.

### Variables

- `y_{i,j,b} in {0,1}` for bay `b`, predecessor `i`, successor `j`.

Trips are time-ordered. `i=0` is a source node meaning that trip `j` is the first trip assigned to bay `b`.

### Objective

Minimize

`sum_{b in B} sum_{j in T} baycost_b y_{0,j,b} + sum_{b in B} sum_{i<j} (baycost_b + cleancost_{i,j}) y_{i,j,b}`

### Constraints

1. Each selected trip has exactly one incoming arc across all bays:

   `sum_{b in B} (y_{0,j,b} + sum_{i<j} y_{i,j,b}) = 1`

2. Each bay starts at most one chain:

   `sum_{j in T} y_{0,j,b} <= 1`

3. On each bay, a trip has at most one outgoing successor and only if it is assigned to that bay:

   `sum_{j>i} y_{i,j,b} <= y_{0,i,b} + sum_{h<i} y_{h,i,b}`

4. Arcs `(i,j,b)` exist only if both trips are bay-compatible and the slot-start gap is at least
   `loading_duration + cleantime_{i,j}`.

Because arcs only point forward in time, this network formulation is acyclic and needs no subtour constraints.

## Exported solution

From the chosen master candidates and the bay-assignment chains:

- `vehicle_id`, `trip_id`, `bay_id`, `load_start_min`;
- exact stop sequence with propagated arrivals and departures;
- exact deliveries by order, stop sequence, compartment, and liters.

All exported quantities are written with up to `1e-8` precision.

import time, importlib.util, sys
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('mysolve', '/app/submissions/solve.py')
mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
start=time.time(); inst=mod.load_instance(); print('load', time.time()-start)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import csv
import json
import math
import os
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum


BASE_DIR = Path("/app")
DATA_DIR = BASE_DIR / "data"
SUBMISSIONS_DIR = BASE_DIR / "submissions"
SOLUTION_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solution.json"
LOG_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solve_log.md"

EPS = 1e-8
FEAS_EPS = 1e-6
CLASS_RANK = {"S": 0, "M": 1, "H": 2}
GROUP_RANK = {"M": 0, "R": 1, "F": 2}
GROUP_NAME = {0: "M", 1: "R", 2: "F"}
DEPOT_ID = "DEPOT"
RESTORATION_BUNDLES = [
    ("S01", "S03"),
    ("S02", "S31"),
    ("S04", "S32"),

    ("S25", "S26"),
]


def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        return list(csv.DictReader(handle))


def read_json(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        return json.load(handle)


def round8(value):
    return float(f"{value:.8f}")


def powerset_masks(length):
    for mask in range(1, 1 << length):
        yield mask


def haversine_km(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):
    radius_km = 6371.0
    p = math.pi / 180.0
    lat1 *= p
    lon1 *= p
    lat2 *= p
    lon2 *= p
    dlat = lat2 - lat1
    dlon = lon2 - lon1
    a = (
        math.sin(dlat / 2.0) ** 2
        + math.cos(lat1) * math.cos(lat2) * math.sin(dlon / 2.0) ** 2
    )
    return 2.0 * radius_km * math.asin(math.sqrt(a))


def bitmask_subset(a, b):
    return (a | b) == b


def station_sort_key(station_id, stations):
    row = stations[station_id]
    return (row["district"], station_id)


def choose_single_pattern(patterns):
    return min(
        patterns,
        key=lambda p: (len(p["used_compartments"]), p["gmax"], -p["gmin"], p["used_mask"]),
    )


def choose_pair_patterns(patterns1, patterns2):
    best = None
    best_score = None
    for p1 in patterns1:
        for p2 in patterns2:
            if p1["used_mask"] & p2["used_mask"]:
                continue
            if p2["gmin"] < p1["gmax"]:
                continue
            score = (
                len(p1["used_compartments"]) + len(p2["used_compartments"]),
                p1["gmax"],
                -p2["gmin"],
                p1["used_mask"],
                p2["used_mask"],
            )
            if best is None or score < best_score:
                best = (p1, p2)
                best_score = score
    return best


def edmonds_karp_assignment(compartments, orders):
    source = "SRC"
    sink = "SNK"
    adjacency = defaultdict(list)
    capacity = {}

    def add_edge(u, v, cap):
        if (u, v) not in capacity:
            adjacency[u].append(v)
            adjacency[v].append(u)
            capacity[(u, v)] = 0.0
            capacity[(v, u)] = 0.0
        capacity[(u, v)] += cap

    total_demand = 0.0
    order_nodes = []
    comp_nodes = []
    for comp in compartments:
        node = f"C::{comp['compartment_id']}"
        comp_nodes.append((node, comp))
        add_edge(source, node, comp["capacity_liters"])
    for order in orders:
        node = f"O::{order['order_id']}"
        order_nodes.append((node, order))
        add_edge(node, sink, order["demand_liters"])
        total_demand += order["demand_liters"]
    for comp_node, comp in comp_nodes:
        for order_node, order in order_nodes:
            if order["product_id"] in comp["compatible_products"]:
                add_edge(comp_node, order_node, total_demand)

    flow = 0.0
    while True:
        parent = {source: None}
        queue = [source]
        while queue and sink not in parent:
            current = queue.pop(0)
            for nxt in adjacency[current]:
                if nxt in parent:
                    continue
                if capacity[(current, nxt)] <= FEAS_EPS:
                    continue
                parent[nxt] = current
                queue.append(nxt)
                if nxt == sink:
                    break
        if sink not in parent:
            break

        augment = float("inf")
        node = sink
        while node != source:
            prev = parent[node]
            augment = min(augment, capacity[(prev, node)])
            node = prev

        node = sink
        while node != source:
            prev = parent[node]
            capacity[(prev, node)] -= augment
            capacity[(node, prev)] += augment
            node = prev
        flow += augment

    if flow + FEAS_EPS < total_demand:
        return None

    assignment = defaultdict(dict)
    for comp_node, comp in comp_nodes:
        for order_node, order in order_nodes:
            reverse_flow = capacity.get((order_node, comp_node), 0.0)
            if reverse_flow > FEAS_EPS:
                assignment[order["order_id"]][comp["compartment_id"]] = reverse_flow
    return assignment


def load_instance():
    config = read_json(DATA_DIR / "config.json")

    products = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "products.csv"):
        products[row["product_id"]] = {
            "product_id": row["product_id"],
            "description": row["description"],
            "density": float(row["density_kg_per_liter"]),
            "hose_family": row["hose_family"],
        }

    vehicles = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "vehicles.csv"):
        vehicles[row["vehicle_id"]] = {
            "vehicle_id": row["vehicle_id"],
            "vehicle_class": row["vehicle_class"],
            "class_rank": CLASS_RANK[row["vehicle_class"]],
            "max_volume_liters": float(row["max_volume_liters"]),
            "max_payload_kg": float(row["max_payload_kg"]),
            "max_trips": int(row["max_trips"]),
            "work_limit_min": float(row["work_limit_min"]),
            "fixed_trip_cost": float(row["fixed_trip_cost"]),
            "cost_per_km": float(row["cost_per_km"]),
            "allowed_station_classes": set(row["allowed_station_classes"].split("|")),
            "compartments": [],
        }

    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "compartments.csv"):
        vehicles[row["vehicle_id"]]["compartments"].append(
            {
                "compartment_id": row["compartment_id"],
                "group": row["group"],
                "group_rank": GROUP_RANK[row["group"]],
                "capacity_liters": float(row["capacity_liters"]),
                "compatible_products": set(row["compatible_products"].split("|")),
            }
        )

    for vehicle in vehicles.values():
        vehicle["compartments"].sort(
            key=lambda c: (c["group_rank"], -c["capacity_liters"], c["compartment_id"])
        )

    stations = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "stations.csv"):
        stations[row["station_id"]] = {
            "station_id": row["station_id"],
            "station_name": row["station_name"],
            "lat": float(row["lat"]),
            "lon": float(row["lon"]),
            "access_class": row["access_class"],
            "earliest_min": float(row["earliest_min"]),
            "preferred_latest_min": float(row["preferred_latest_min"]),
            "latest_min": float(row["latest_min"]),
            "fixed_service_min": float(row["fixed_service_min"]),
            "split_allowed": row["split_allowed"] == "1",
            "risk_zone": row["risk_zone"],
            "district": row["district"],
        }

    orders = {}
    station_orders = defaultdict(list)
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "orders.csv"):

/bin/bash -lc "PYTHONPATH=/opt/or-deps python - <<'PY'
import json,csv
from collections import defaultdict
from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum
sol=json.load(open('/app/submissions/solution.json'))
vehicles={r['vehicle_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/vehicles.csv'))}
comps=defaultdict(list)
for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/compartments.csv')):
    comps[r['vehicle_id']].append(r)
orders={r['order_id']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open('/app/data/orders.csv'))}
grank={'M':0,'R':1,'F':2}
for trip in sol['trips']:
    v=trip['vehicle_id']
    trip_orders=[]
    stop_nodes={i+1:s['node_id'] for i,s in enumerate(trip['stops'])}
    stop_seq_by_order={d['order_id']:d['stop_sequence'] for d in trip['deliveries']}
    for oid,seq in sorted(stop_seq_by_order.items(), key=lambda x:(x[1],x[0])):
        trip_orders.append((oid, seq, orders[oid]['product_id'], float(orders[oid]['demand_liters'])))
    model=Model(); model.setParam('display/verblevel',0)
    y={}; q={}
    for c in comps[v]:
        cid=c['compartment_id']
        for oid,seq,p,d in trip_orders:
            if p in c['compatible_products'].split('|'):
                y[cid,oid]=model.addVar(vtype='B', name=f'y[{cid},{oid}]')
                q[cid,oid]=model.addVar(lb=0.0, ub=float(c['capacity_liters']), name=f'q[{cid},{oid}]')
                model.addCons(q[cid,oid] <= float(c['capacity_liters'])*y[cid,oid])
    for c in comps[v]:
        cid=c['compartment_id']
        model.addCons(quicksum(y[cid,oid] for oid,_,_,_ in trip_orders if (cid,oid) in y) <= 1)
    for oid,seq,p,d in trip_orders:
        model.addCons(quicksum(q[cid,oid] for c in comps[v] for cid in [c['compartment_id']] if (cid,oid) in q) == d)
    # precedence: later stop compartments must have group >= any earlier used group
    for c1 in comps[v]:
        cid1=c1['compartment_id']; g1=grank[c1['group']]
        for c2 in comps[v]:
            cid2=c2['compartment_id']; g2=grank[c2['group']]
            for oid1,seq1,_,_ in trip_orders:
                for oid2,seq2,_,_ in trip_orders:
                    if seq1 < seq2 and g1 > g2 and (cid1,oid1) in y and (cid2,oid2) in y:
                        model.addCons(y[cid1,oid1] + y[cid2,oid2] <= 1)
    model.setObjective(0.0)
    model.optimize()
    print(trip['trip_id'], model.getStatus(), model.getNSols())
    if model.getNSols()==0:
        print('trip orders', trip_orders)
PY"
